2.2    Exact normal fiber chart and coarea
Let Xm , Ym be exponentially weighted Banach coordinate spaces and let

                    L : Xm → Ym ,         P : Ym → Xm ,              LP = I,        ∥P ∥m = p < 1.

Put K = ker L and write

                            B(x) = Lx + N (x),              N (0) = 0,       DN (0) = 0.

Theorem 2.5 (Normal fiber chart). For w ∈ Ym and ζ ∈ K, consider
                                       F (y; w, ζ) = y + N (P y + ζ) − w.
Assume on a product ball that
                                       η := sup ∥DN (P y + ζ)P ∥m < 1.
Then there is a unique analytic solution y = T (w, ζ) and
                                            PN (w, ζ) := P T (w, ζ) + ζ
is an exact fiber chart:
                                                  B(PN (w, ζ)) = w.
If A = I + DN (PN )P , then
                                Dw T = A− 1,            Dζ T = −A− 1DN (PN )|K ,
and hence
                           Dw PN = P A− 1,           Dζ PN = IK − P A− 1DN (PN )|K .
All derivatives through order three are finite sums of products of A− 1, P, D2 N, D3 N and are exponentially
local in the same Banach matrix algebra.
Proof. For fixed (w, ζ), the map
                                                 y 7→ w − N (P y + ζ)
is an η-contraction on a sufficiently chosen invariant ball. Banach’s fixed-point theorem gives T . The
exact fiber identity follows by substitution. Differentiating
                                              T + N (P T + ζ) = w
gives the displayed first derivatives. Repeated differentiation gives the second- and third-order Bell-
polynomial formulas. Since ∥A− 1∥ ≤ (1 − η)− 1, every term is bounded in the same exponential matrix
algebra.

Proof. The chart factors as

2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.
Proposition 2.9 (Model-specific nonlinear normal section for the forty-nine-path block). Let

                             pm := ∥P0 ∥Km < 0.546,           C2 := CN,2 (m; η0 , η1 ),

and choose
                                                               1
                                                                    
                                                          η0
                                           ρm = min          ,           .

For every retained coordinate w with ∥w∥Km ≤ ρm , the retained-space equation

                                               y + N (P0 y) = w

has a unique solution y = T (w, 0) in ∥y∥Km ≤ 2ρm . The zero-detail normal section

                                              S(w) := P0 T (w, 0)

satisfies
                                  B(S(w)) = w,        ∥S(w)∥Km ≤ 2pm ρm .
It is analytic and exponentially quasi-local, and its first three marked derivatives have volume- and
depth-independent bounds. The same conclusion holds on the aligned-reflection templates after taking the
minimum radius over that finite family.
Proof. For fixed w, define
                                            Φw (y) := w − N (P0 y).
If ∥y∥ ≤ 2ρm , then ∥P0 y∥ ≤ η0 /2. Since DN (0) = 0, Lemma 2.8 gives
                                                                                 pm  1

Analyticity follows from the analytic implicit-function theorem. Differentiation through order three gives
finite Bell polynomials in
                               (I + DN (S)P0 )−1 , P0 , D2 N, D3 N.
The inverse norm is below two and the other factors are bounded by Theorem 2.4 and Lemma 2.8.

3     Charged chart complement and irregular sector
          √
Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold
in this section and in the following two irregular-sector sections; the rooted support exponent remains
denoted by κ elsewhere.
   For an enlarged finite tube cell Q+ define the faithful fine Wilson charge and retained coherent deficit
by the same representation-normalized plaquette function used in the microscopic measure:
                                     X                              X
                        AQ+ (U ) =      wG,ρ (Up ),     DQ (U ) =       wG,ρ (Wp′ ).
                                    p⊂Q+                            p′ ⊂Q′

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Lemma 3.2 (Simultaneous selected-anchor derivative). Select the first fine link of every straight two-link
anchor as the anchor variable; these links are distinct. Hold every remaining fine link in a detail list Z,
and write the simultaneous retained-coordinate map in product exponential charts as
                                                 b Z : a 7−→ w.

                                    b Z (a) = ϑ(I + KZ (a)),            7

The same row and column estimates hold after the fixed marked derivatives used below.
Proof. At the flat field, the selected straight input contributes ϑI. The cross-anchor cancellation can be
read directly from the flat forty-nine-path symbol. Its diagonal detour part is proportional to
                                                      kµ Y
                                              cos          cos kλ ,
                                                       2

so in position space every detour coefficient has an odd displacement in all three transverse directions
relative to the selected all-even anchor column. For µ ̸= ν, the factor
                                        kν

has an odd µ displacement. Thus no detour term lands on another selected anchor link; the orientation

the common weak threshold makes the two Schur sums at most 1/4. Chart-trivialization and every fixed
mark add only another finite-incidence O(g(1 + Rg )) term.

Theorem 3.3 (Fixed-chart global anchor coordinate). For fixed details Z, the map B
                                                                                 b Z is bi-Lipschitz
on the convex product chart and a diffeomorphism onto its image. More precisely,
                             3                      b Z (b)∥2 ≤ ϑ 5 ∥a − b∥2 .

                            ϑ ∥a − b∥2 ≤ ∥B
                             4                                    4
Every principal subproblem obtained by fixing the exterior anchors has the same lower Lipschitz bound
and a volume-independent inverse derivative.
Proof. For v = a − b, integrate the derivative along the segment from b to a. Schur’s test gives
∥KZ ∥2→2 ≤ 1/4, proving both bounds. The lower bound gives injectivity; the inverse-function theorem
gives the smooth inverse. A principal submatrix of KZ has no larger row or column sum, so the same proof
applies with exterior anchors fixed. Higher fixed marked derivatives follow by differentiating (I + KZ )−1
and using its Neumann series.
Theorem 3.4 (Finite-cell retention and chart charge). There are constants Cret < ∞, cch > 0, and
tch > 0, depending only on the fixed cell atlas, such that

                                            DQ (U ) ≤ Cret AQ+ (U )

for every fine configuration. Moreover,

                                   TQ (U ) ≥ tch /4     =⇒     AQ+ (U ) ≥ cch .

The constants are independent of total volume, ultraviolet depth, periodic wrapping, and the aligned-
reflection template.
Proof. Use a boundary-rooted tree gauge on the fixed finite contractible cell. By Lemma 3.1, the zero set
of AQ+ is precisely the flat set. On that set all common-endpoint paths agree, hence DQ = TQ = 0. Near
the flat set, the representation-normalized quadratic comparison and the grounded finite-cell Poincaré
inequality give
                                          AQ+ ≍ ∥dX∥2 ≍ ∥X∥2 ,
while smoothness of the finite path atlas and the fact that every retained plaquette word equals the
identity on the flat set give DQ + TQ ≤ C∥X∥2 . This proves the retention inequality on one neighborhood
of the flat set.
   Remove a smaller such neighborhood. On the resulting compact set, AQ+ has a strictly positive
minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This


                                                         11
proves the global displayed inequality without extending a quotient through its zero set. Finally choose
tch inside the chart reserve. The closed set {TQ ≥ tch /4} is disjoint from the flat set; compactness and
Lemma 3.1 give a positive minimum cch of AQ+ on it. Taking the finite maximum and minimum over the
bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

  Choose smooth decreasing cutoffs γD , γ0 , γ1 ∈ [0, 1] with γ0 ≤ γ1 and

                               γD = 1 (DQ ≤ χ2 /4), γD = 0 (DQ ≥ χ2 ),
                               γ0 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /8), γ0 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch /4),
                               γ1 = 1 (TQ ≤ tch /2), γ1 = 0 (TQ ≥ tch ).

Define the exact labelled partition

pointwise. The labels are used only inside absolute-value expansions and are recombined before reflection

Lemma 3.5 (Charged support of every irregular label). After fixing 0 < χ ≤ χ0 with χ20 ≤ 4cch , every
curvature, collar, or outer label is supported on

                                                 AQ+ (U ) ≥ c0 χ2

for one fixed c0 > 0. Every derivative of a splice cutoff through order 2sG is supported on the same
charged event and has a fixed-template polynomial bound in χ−1 and the fixed chart thresholds.

Proof. On the support of 1 − γD , either DQ ≥ χ2 /4 or a derivative of γD is present. The retention
inequality gives AQ+ ≥ χ2 /(4Cret ). On the collar and outer supports, or on a derivative of γ0 or γ1 , one
has TQ ≥ tch /8 after decreasing the fixed thresholds once. The compactness argument in Theorem 3.4
gives a fixed positive action charge, which dominates c0 χ2 for χ ≤ χ0 . The derivative bounds through
order 2sG follow from the fixed smooth cutoffs, Lemma 3.1, and the finite cell atlas; differentiation changes
neither transition support nor action charge.

   Fix R0 larger than the diameter of every local variable support in one normalized step. For each
labelled bad block Q, choose a finite integration core IQ containing every variable on which its cutoff and
retention estimate depend, and put

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;
2. the geometric reporting lemma;
3. the complete-species allocation cone;
4. the exact dimension-ledger reblocking factor 24−∆ ;
5. a logarithmic-support localization estimate
                           qloc (D, L) ≤ C L−3 + (log L)pD LΓ−δD ,
                                                               
                                                                                      δD → ∞.
Choose κ = µ/2. If the rooted regular atom mass and allocation radius are small enough that
                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,
then one may choose finite D and finite L = 2s so that the stable regular derivative satisfies
                                                 ∥DΦreg |stable ∥ < 1
in the same permanent κ-weighted support norm.
Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric
and regulator cost into a per-vertex constant. The summed BKAR tree recursion is therefore geometric
in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

3.3    Exterior complex tube
Fix the exact symmetric forty-nine-path fixed-G block. Let X be a connected curvature-bad component,
I(X) its fixed integration core, and RX its exterior ring. Use the selected-anchor component chart on
I(X), with every chart Jacobian included in the density.
Lemma 3.12 (Finite word transport). There are fixed radii δout , δin > 0 and a constant Ctr < ∞,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the component anchor solve is holomorphic for

Proof. Every owned plaquette is a finite word in the fixed forty-nine-path atlas. On the smaller tube, the
exponential, logarithm, Karcher solve, and principal-submatrix anchor inverse are holomorphic. Their
derivatives are bounded on the compact closure of each local template tube. There are only finitely many
bulk and aligned-reflection local templates; periodic wrapping does not change a rooted local template.
Summing the finite local row/column incidence bounds gives the displayed estimate.

Lemma 3.15 (Inherited sectorial lifting). Let the incoming regular interaction be holomorphic on the

4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.


                                                        28
  After canonical scale-two rescaling, define C≥j+1 to be the rescaled coarse covariance Cjc . Thus the
notation
                                          C≥j = Cj + C≥j+1
used below means the exact identity of Theorem 4.11, with Cj = Cjd and with the remaining covariance
embedded by Jj before the canonical identification. It is not a declaration of an unconstructed translation-
invariant product slicing.
Corollary 4.12 (Harmonic-extension Wick martingale). For every finite polynomial P in the fine tangent
field,
                           Eζ∼Cjd : P (Jj W + ζ) :C≥j =: (P ◦ Jj )(W ) :Cjc .
                                                       

The identity commutes with every fixed background mark and every prescribed finite family of passive
bounded source marks. The contraction operators commute and C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ . Therefore
                              1
                              2 ∆C d       − 21 ∆C≥j                    − 21 ∆C c
                          e       j    e               P (Jj W + ·) 0 = e      j    (P ◦ Jj )(W ).

The covariance is fixed and independent of the background and source parameters, so those derivatives
commute with the contraction operators.
4.3.4 Exact Wilson–Haar factorization in the nonlinear fiber chart
Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its
exact finite-volume coarea density. On the inner regular chart, write dγj (ζ) for the normalized Gaussian
with covariance Cjd .
Theorem 4.13 (Exact regular-reference identity). At finite volume and before any thermodynamic or
cutoff limit, the inner regular part of one Wilson fiber integral has the exact form
                Z                          Z              Z
                            −1
                   F (U )e−u SW (U ) dU = dW ρ0,j (W ) dγj (ζ) F (PN (W, ζ))e−Vj (W,ζ;u) .

is defined with cj (W ) chosen to remove the empty-support term. Thus the identity is a change of measure,
not a Gaussian approximation. All nonquadratic Wilson terms, the complete field-dependent Haar and
coarea factors, normal-section corrections, inherited interactions, sources, and regular cutoffs remain
visible in Vj or in the charged irregular operators. No Gaussian product factorization across spatial
components is asserted.
Proof. Apply the exact finite-volume normal-fiber and coarea identity to the compact Wilson integral in
the boundary-rooted gauge. Multiply and divide by the strictly positive normalized density of γj . The
displayed formula then follows by the definition of Vj and the once-only normalization cj . The exact
partition into inner, curvature, collar, and outer labels is made before logarithms and is recombined
before reflection positivity is used. Gauge covariance, signed-hypercubic covariance, and aligned-reflection
compatibility follow from covariance of the Karcher map, its normal section, the tree gauge, and the
scalar Feynman precision. Individual artificial Gaussian detail measures are not claimed to be reflection
positive.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local

    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local

Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

stable remainder lies in the kernel of LocD , while every off-diagonal local/remainder block has the same
Taylor-remainder gain. Since
                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),
the discrete covariant localization lemma gives

                                              CD,m (1 + R(L))pD L3−D

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,

4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.


                                                        28
  After canonical scale-two rescaling, define C≥j+1 to be the rescaled coarse covariance Cjc . Thus the
notation
                                          C≥j = Cj + C≥j+1
used below means the exact identity of Theorem 4.11, with Cj = Cjd and with the remaining covariance
embedded by Jj before the canonical identification. It is not a declaration of an unconstructed translation-
invariant product slicing.
Corollary 4.12 (Harmonic-extension Wick martingale). For every finite polynomial P in the fine tangent
field,
                           Eζ∼Cjd : P (Jj W + ζ) :C≥j =: (P ◦ Jj )(W ) :Cjc .
                                                       

The identity commutes with every fixed background mark and every prescribed finite family of passive
bounded source marks. The contraction operators commute and C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ . Therefore
                              1
                              2 ∆C d       − 21 ∆C≥j                    − 21 ∆C c
                          e       j    e               P (Jj W + ·) 0 = e      j    (P ◦ Jj )(W ).

The covariance is fixed and independent of the background and source parameters, so those derivatives
commute with the contraction operators.
4.3.4 Exact Wilson–Haar factorization in the nonlinear fiber chart
Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its
exact finite-volume coarea density. On the inner regular chart, write dγj (ζ) for the normalized Gaussian
with covariance Cjd .
Theorem 4.13 (Exact regular-reference identity). At finite volume and before any thermodynamic or
cutoff limit, the inner regular part of one Wilson fiber integral has the exact form
                Z                          Z              Z

                   F (U )e−u SW (U ) dU = dW ρ0,j (W ) dγj (ζ) F (PN (W, ζ))e−Vj (W,ζ;u) .

not a Gaussian approximation. All nonquadratic Wilson terms, the complete field-dependent Haar and

visible in Vj or in the charged irregular operators. No Gaussian product factorization across spatial

Proof. Apply the exact finite-volume normal-fiber and coarea identity to the compact Wilson integral in
the boundary-rooted gauge. Multiply and divide by the strictly positive normalized density of γj . The
displayed formula then follows by the definition of Vj and the once-only normalization cj . The exact
partition into inner, curvature, collar, and outer labels is made before logarithms and is recombined
before reflection positivity is used. Gauge covariance, signed-hypercubic covariance, and aligned-reflection
compatibility follow from covariance of the Karcher map, its normal section, the tree gauge, and the
scalar Feynman precision. Individual artificial Gaussian detail measures are not claimed to be reflection
positive.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local

    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local

Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.

     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization

Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent

                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.

                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives

does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher

finite fixed-template suprema.

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).

templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1
                                          KL (x, y) ≥ ϵM
                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates

                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.

No marginal density is logarithmized.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.

Lemma 6.23 (Tri-graded optical/source upgrade). In the exact factorization of Theorem 6.10, the
normalized one-pivot logarithmic scores, VA,J , zA,J = e−VA,J − 1, the exact positive denominators, and

Proof. Differentiate the exact compact factorization before integrating the optical field. At this call site ζ

and the finite one-core normalizations already present in the exact factorization; it does not create an
additional BKAR covariance derivative.
    For the reference, regular, coarea, determinant, and Gaussian-normalization factors, apply Lemma 6.22

with |α| ≤ 2sG , r ≤ 3, and q ≤ 2. Transition and outer labels are finite-dimensional smooth densities on

the component size and is absorbed by the bad-action exponential.
   The maps V 7→ e−V − 1, multiplication, inversion of the strictly positive scalar denominator, and
the normalized logarithmic-score map are analytic maps of this finite-order tri-graded Banach algebra.
Leibniz, Faà di Bruno, and the quotient rule are paid by the three factorial weights in (56). Since G
is fixed, the derivative orders 2sG , three, and two are fixed finite integers, so the resulting constant is
uniform in all declared limits. This proves the stated tri-graded row without identifying an optical field
derivative with a passive source derivative.

Lemma 6.25 (Complete compact-flux classification and canonical exploration). Fix ζ and take the
Peter–Weyl decomposition of the complete compact incidence network generated by the exact pushforward
under BL . Integrate all compact variables coefficientwise. A connected history is compact-vacuum only
when every compact cut separating distinct mixed optical/source vertices carries the trivial representation.
After removal of already reported physical cross-core connectors, such a history factorizes into one-core
expectations or empty-support scalars and is assigned by the exact owner map to the normalized one-core
references or the pre-existing regular/Gaussian inventory.
   Every remaining history, including a multi-leg singlet with trivial visible coarse label but nontrivial
internal flux, has a deterministic visited-set exploration. Order incident half-edges before marking an
unoriented edge inspected. Discovery edges form a rooted spanning tree; each merger, reconnection, shared
tail, cycle, seam, reflection, or wrapping edge is a uniquely owned closure edge. The record reconstructs
the occupied geometric network without duplicating shared continuations.

Proof. Vacuum factorization follows by deleting the reported physical connectors and applying Fubini
across every trivial compact cut. Otherwise the least mixed cut, least nontrivial representation label, and
least oriented half-edge choose the root and first edge. Deterministic depth-first exploration then classifies
every occupied unoriented edge exactly once as a discovery or closure edge. A composite singlet is retained
whenever any internal separating cut is nontrivial, independently of the visible output label.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into

Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each

Theorem 7.2 (Retained probe-root influence row). For every bounded centered probe in a fixed matching-

Theorem 7.3 (Exact local-source retention and Gate–E closure). Let F be a bounded local matching

                             DJq ⟨F ; B⟩c J=0 ≤ CF,B,q e−mE r ,     q = 0, 1, 2.                      (107)

7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
in the explicit inventory AJG .
Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
are removed by the connected logarithm.
Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

Corollary 7.8 (No additional acoustic path class). The exact vertex inventory used in Theorem 6.43 ad-
mits no fourth root-to-root path class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge. Consequently
the boundary-to-center estimates (104) and (107) require no additional terminal Witten, acoustic-channel,
or retained-Gaussian gap hypothesis.
Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.
Apply Theorem 7.7 to exclude the only remaining acoustic fourth-class concern.

Proof. Each coarse value is a convex average of the fine values, proving the first inequality. The second

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put
x = mw (d − CR )+ . The scale sum is bounded by C r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1,
and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.

Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose

   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Theorem 7.2 (Retained probe-root influence row). For every bounded centered probe in a fixed matching-
scale source buffer, the exact normalized response satisfies the zero-, one-, and two-source weighted row
and column bounds of (106).
Proof. Insert the probe as one of the two declared root vertices before applying Theorem 6.43. The

joint logarithm.
Theorem 7.3 (Exact local-source retention and Gate–E closure). Let F be a bounded local matching
observable and let B be any bounded registered exterior perturbation supported at distance at least r from
F . Through source order two,

                             DJq ⟨F ; B⟩c J=0 ≤ CF,B,q e−mE r ,     q = 0, 1, 2.                      (107)

The same estimate holds for a finite family of compact-boundary or optical chart perturbations whose

statement consumed by the source forest.
Proof. Use F and B as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The owner inventory is exhaustive at the

of compact boundary data, differentiation along a unit-speed group geodesic inserts its centered local
score as the exterior root; for an admitted optical chart interpolation the affine derivative inserts the
corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the
exact regular/irregular owner decomposition. Integrating the finite interpolation gives the same estimate.
No claim for arbitrary uncontrolled boundary measures is needed downstream.

7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
in the explicit inventory AJG .
Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
are removed by the connected logarithm.
Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradiction. The only possible remaining concern would be an undeclared
massless retained covariance, which is excluded by Lemma 7.5.

Corollary 7.8 (No additional acoustic path class). The exact vertex inventory used in Theorem 6.43 ad-
mits no fourth root-to-root path class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge. Consequently
the boundary-to-center estimates (104) and (107) require no additional terminal Witten, acoustic-channel,
or retained-Gaussian gap hypothesis.
Proof. The vertex inventory is already exhaustive by Lemma 6.7, Proposition 6.8, and Corollary 6.40.
Apply Theorem 7.7 to exclude the only remaining acoustic fourth-class concern.

Lemma 8.4 (Weak-scale connector). Fix two physical source buffers A, B and let d = dist(A, B). The

Lemma 8.5 (Matching-scale spacetime connector). For bounded matching-scale cylinders F, G supported

                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.
Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned
exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw . Finer decorations attached to
its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put

                                             dw := CR + m−1
                                                         w .



                                                     75
For d ≥ dw ,                   X
                                      Ce−c0 | log σ|(d−CR )/ℓj ≤ C ′ e−mw (d−CR )/2 .                  (116)
                               j≥jw

For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for
bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields.
Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected
tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional-
logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes
automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer
decoration independently of the number of activePnonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw ) ℓjw , put
x = mw (d − CR )+ . The scale sum is bounded by C r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1,
and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged
physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors;
they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement.

Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose

take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
   For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-

regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection

Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time

The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization
factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along
the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

8.2    Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
     even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
     tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
      presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar , and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.
